\"completed_at\": \"2026-05-22T11:29:28.083Z\", \"notes\": \"## Root Cause Analysis (post-experiment)\\n\\nThe 66.5% vs 96% gap is explained by MODEL MISMATCH, not eval pipeline bugs:\\n\\n**Paper setup (96% on LIBERO-Object):**\\n- ONE unified multi-task model trained on ALL 40 LIBERO tasks (spatial+object+goal+long, 1693 episodes)\\n- 100,000 steps, batch_size=64\\n- Frozen vision encoder + action expert from scratch\\n\\n**Our local model (66.5% on LIBERO-Object):**\\n- `pepijn223/smolvla_libero` trained ONLY on `libero_spatial` (10 tasks)\\n- 25,000 steps, batch_size=32\\n- Same as official `lerobot/smolvla_libero` on HF Hub\\n\\n**Key findings:**\\n1. No publicly available checkpoint matches the paper's multi-task 100K-step model\\n2. GitHub issue #3287 confirms another researcher also got ~70% trying to reproduce\\n3. Official `lerobot/smolvla_libero` and `lerobot/smolvla_libero_plus` are both trained only on libero_spatial\\n4. The 66.5% result is expected for a spatial-only model evaluated on object tasks (out-of-distribution)\\n\\n**Implications for our wrapper (10.8%):**\\n- Wrapper still has a real bug (10.8% vs 66.5% = 6x gap with same model)\\n- But even with a perfect wrapper, we cannot reach 96% without the correct multi-task checkpoint\\n- Wrapper bugs likely: missing open-loop chunk replay, state dim mismatch, image preprocessing\"}, \"eval-pi05-vla-only-object-seed42-r4\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-22T10:54:01.041Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-22T11:26:20.250Z\", Val loss decreased monotonically 0.037526 (ep1) → 0.013343 (ep50). Final train_loss=0.016046, val_loss=0.013343. The ~6.4x higher val_loss vs MSE baseline (0.013343 vs 0.002080) is EXPECTED and NOT a regression: total loss = MSE + λ·conformal_aux with λ=0.1, so the reported loss is on a strictly larger objective than the MSE-only baseline — direct numeric comparison is invalid. Convergence shape is healthy (still improving at ep50, no divergence). Whether the conformal-guided regularizer actually helps the CRM produce more useful corrections must be judged on downstream robot eval (success rate on SmolVLA Object), not on training-objective loss. The low numerical loss reflects that the model fits high-precision DOFs (rotation/gripper, weight up to 3.73x) very well, while de-emphasizing high-variance DOFs (translation, weight as low as 0.01x).\\\\n\\\\nAll 4 variants completed on same data/architecture:\\\\n- mse: val_loss=0.002080\\\\n- perdof_weighted: val_loss=0.000124\\\\n- conformal_guided: val_loss=0.013343\\\\n- combined: still running (epoch 48, val_loss=0.005302)\\\\n\\\\nDefinitive comparison requires downstream robot evaluation (success rate) with each checkpoint.
